MEDCOM CONNECT
Expanded Website Contentfor Every Card and Section
A detailed, original content specification covering all 56 website pages, hero copy, section descriptions, individual card copy, calls to action, interaction guidance and CMS ownership.
What has been added in this expanded version
Every original section from the prior blueprint is now presented as a complete content module. Each module includes a visitor-facing heading, a detailed explanation of its purpose, publishable on-card copy, an action label and practical guidance for the live experience. This means the development and content team can build a page without having to guess what a card should say.
Originality and publishing rule
All copy has been developed as an original MedCom Connect concept. Before launch, replace working-brand placeholders, names, legal statements, prices, event data, membership eligibility, photos, testimonials, policies, metrics and all local facts with client-approved information. Do not reuse a competitor’s wording, images, programme names or distinctive layouts.
How to Use This Content Specification
Use the document in this order: first build the hierarchy and global components; then implement each page’s hero and content modules; finally connect the detailed actions to CMS entries, forms, account permissions and live data. The content is intentionally written in a light, editorial and professional tone.
Global light-theme rule
The website must remain bright and refined: white backgrounds, pale blue hero panels, soft mint sections, cream information callouts and occasional pale lilac or blush accents. Deep navy/blue is for readable text only. Do not use black footers, dark hero panels, dark gradients or heavy shadow effects.
Global header copy
Primary navigation: Membership, Community, Learning, Career, Knowledge, Events, About, Get Involved. Utility actions: Search, Join MedCom Connect, Sign in. Each dropdown must include a short descriptive sentence so navigation feels like guidance, not only a list of links.
Global footer copy
Footer statement: “MedCom Connect brings people and practice together so healthcare communication can be clearer, more responsible and more useful.” Footer columns: Explore; Member Support; Learning, Knowledge and Events; About and Trust. Newsletter copy: “Choose the updates that help you stay connected to useful learning, community activity and professional opportunities.”
Website Hierarchy at a Glance
The page inventory below matches the complete information architecture. Dynamic item pages such as individual course, job, article, event and directory profile pages are built once as templates and rendered from CMS records.

01. Homepage
Create an immediate, welcoming understanding of the association as a practical professional home, with clear routes for learning, connection, career development and contribution.
Primary audience
Membership
Primary CTA
Explore membership
Secondary CTA
Browse upcoming learning
HERO CONTENT - COPY TO PLACE ON THE PAGE
Where medical communicators learn, connect and make a bigger difference.
MedCom Connect brings together people who turn complex evidence into clear, responsible communication. Build practical skills, find a trusted community and take part in work that improves how healthcare knowledge is shared.
DETAILED CONTENT MODULES
1. Hero and audience pathways
This part of the page helps you make a membership decision without hidden detail or pressure. Use a full-width pale-blue hero with natural-light imagery of a workshop discussion, a remote learning moment, a writing desk and a networking conversation. Add a rounded site-search field labelled “What would you like to explore?” The content should remain concise on the live page, but it must answer the practical question behind “what is this for?” before the user is asked to click.
CARD 1
Hero and audience pathways: what visitors should understand
Use this card to give visitors a direct, useful explanation of hero and audience pathways.
Use a full-width pale-blue hero with natural-light imagery of a workshop discussion, a remote learning moment, a writing desk and a networking conversation. Add a rounded site-search field labelled “What would you like to explore?” In the live website, place this explanation in plain language before any filters, forms or dense lists. The visitor should be able to understand the value of this section even if they only read this card.
Exact on-card copy: “Hero and audience pathways should make the next professional choice easier. Explore the available options, understand what each one is designed for and choose the route that best fits the work you are doing now.”
Action: Explore membership options
CARD 2
Hero and audience pathways: how it works in practice
Use a supporting card to remove uncertainty about participation, access or next steps.
Show three audience shortcuts: “I am new to medical communication”, “I want to develop specialist skills”, and “I am looking for an expert, role or opportunity”. Keep two prominent CTA buttons in the hero. Explain the practical experience: what the visitor can browse, what information will be visible, what is optional, and where a member or guest may need to sign in. Keep this explanation supportive rather than procedural.
Exact on-card copy: “You do not need to work everything out at once. Start with the information that is relevant to you, compare the choices available and return whenever you are ready to take the next step.”
Action: Link to: Explore membership options
CARD 3
Hero and audience pathways: confidence and next action
Use a final, smaller card or CTA strip to reassure the visitor and offer one specific next move.
Make it clear that a visitor can continue without committing to more than they are ready for. Provide a relevant FAQ, contact/support route or saved-item option where useful. This is especially important for Homepage, where people may be comparing choices or handling a time-sensitive question.
Exact on-card copy: “Need a little more context before you decide? Explore the related guidance, save this for later or contact the team for help choosing the right route.”
Action: Primary CTA: Explore membership options; secondary link: Contact support
Implementation and layout note
Show three audience shortcuts: “I am new to medical communication”, “I want to develop specialist skills”, and “I am looking for an expert, role or opportunity”. Keep two prominent CTA buttons in the hero.

Page-level quality check
Before publishing Homepage, confirm that all calls to action are live, the page is usable on mobile, any time-sensitive content is current, access labels are accurate, forms have confirmation states, and no protected or partner content is presented as independent editorial guidance.
02. Membership Hub
Position membership as lasting professional support, not as a simple transactional subscription.
Primary audience
Membership
Primary CTA
Join now
Secondary CTA
Compare membership types
HERO CONTENT - COPY TO PLACE ON THE PAGE
Membership that moves with your career.
Whether you are exploring the field, growing a specialist practice or leading a team, membership gives you practical learning, useful connections and room to contribute.
DETAILED CONTENT MODULES
1. Why join
This part of the page helps you make a membership decision without hidden detail or pressure. Lead with: “Your work changes. Your professional network should keep up.” Explain that members can build skills, connect across roles, access selected tools and bring their own voice into the community. The content should remain concise on the live page, but it must answer the practical question behind “what is this for?” before the user is asked to click.

Make it clear that a visitor can continue without committing to more than they are ready for. Provide a relevant FAQ, contact/support route or saved-item option where useful. This is especially important for Courses and Workshops, where people may be comparing choices or handling a time-sensitive question.
Exact on-card copy: “Need a little more context before you decide? Explore the related guidance, save this for later or contact the team for help choosing the right route.”
Action: Primary CTA: Browse learning; secondary link: Contact support
Implementation and layout note
Avoid a dead-end result page.
Page-level quality check
Before publishing Courses and Workshops, confirm that all calls to action are live, the page is usable on mobile, any time-sensitive content is current, access labels are accurate, forms have confirmation states, and no protected or partner content is presented as independent editorial guidance.

1. Featured reading

Feature three original articles with title, abstract, category, author and estimated reading time. The content should remain concise on the live page, but it must answer the practical question behind “what is this for?” before the user is asked to click.
CARD 1
Featured reading: what visitors should understand
Use this card to give visitors a direct, useful explanation of featured reading.
Feature three original articles with title, abstract, category, author and estimated reading time. In the live website, place this explanation in plain language before any filters, forms or dense lists. The visitor should be able to understand the value of this section even if they only read this card.
Exact on-card copy: “Featured reading should make the next professional choice easier. Explore the available options, understand what each one is designed for and choose the route that best fits the work you are doing now.”
Action: Explore related guidance
CARD 2
Featured reading: how it works in practice
Use a supporting card to remove uncertainty about participation, access or next steps.
Suggested themes: audience-friendly evidence narratives; better review cycles; responsible use of assistive tools. Explain the practical experience: what the visitor can browse, what information will be visible, what is optional, and where a member or guest may need to sign in. Keep this explanation supportive rather than procedural.
Exact on-card copy: “You do not need to work everything out at once. Start with the information that is relevant to you, compare the choices available and return whenever you are ready to take the next step.”
Action: Link to: Explore related guidance
CARD 3
Featured reading: confidence and next action
Use a final, smaller card or CTA strip to reassure the visitor and offer one specific next move.
Make it clear that a visitor can continue without committing to more than they are ready for. Provide a relevant FAQ, contact/support route or saved-item option where useful. This is especially important for Knowledge Hub, where people may be comparing choices or handling a time-sensitive question.
Exact on-card copy: “Need a little more context before you decide? Explore the related guidance, save this for later or contact the team for help choosing the right route.”
Action: Primary CTA: Explore related guidance; secondary link: Contact support
Implementation and layout note
Suggested themes: audience-friendly evidence narratives; better review cycles; responsible use of assistive tools.

Appendix: Content Operations and Quality Controls
This section turns the copy deck into an operating website. It ensures the site remains current, usable and trustworthy after launch.
Weekly update rhythm
Review live event availability, course registrations, bookings, waitlists, job closing dates, broken links, form routing and urgent news. Remove or label expired items immediately.
Monthly update rhythm
Refresh homepage features, recommendations, course panels, event highlights, resource spotlights, member stories, partnership labels and newsletter prompts. Review abandoned join or checkout journeys.
Quarterly update rhythm
Review membership routes, community listings, chapter activity, directory profiles, standards summaries, active policies, accessibility feedback, support-page FAQs and page-owner status.
Annual update rhythm
Review mission and values, legal notices, privacy/cookie disclosures, governance pages, account-security wording, archive retention rules, contributor guidance and SEO metadata.
Accessibility and trust controls
Every module must use semantic headings, visible field labels, keyboard-friendly interactions, sufficient text contrast, descriptive links, text alternatives and clear error/success states. Where a page uses data, member access or commercial relationships, publish the context and limitation in plain language.
Launch Checklist
All 56 pages and all dynamic templates are implemented, routed and reachable from the hierarchy.
Every visible card has its full title, description, CTA and destination. No card contains only a heading with no explanation.
All working-brand placeholders, data, dates, fees, legal text, contacts, people, images and policies have been replaced with approved information.
The design uses a consistent light theme and maintains accessible contrast without dark sections.
All forms have privacy text, validation, confirmation and an internal routing owner.
Search, filters, account access, directory visibility, course booking, event registration, job expiry and checkout flows are fully tested.
Editorial and partnership content are clearly separated and partnership labels are visible where relevant.
The organisation has named owners for membership, learning, community, career, knowledge, events, policies, account support and content review.
